176	## baselines
177	
178	### baselines.RandomPlayer: Random baseline
179	`class RandomPlayer(Agent): def __init__(self, n_actions: int, *, seed: int)`
180	- **Invariants:** uniform over actions whatever `explore` says (BASE-01). `learn` is a no-op.
181	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.RandomPlayer/ · **Cases:** a replay double of 10 seeded `act` calls
182	- **Used by:** lab; PL-03, PL-04
183	
184	### baselines.ScriptedPlayer: Scripted baseline
185	`class ScriptedPlayer(Agent): def __init__(self) -> None`
186	- **Invariants:** BASE-02 on the compact view, deterministic. Ties go straight, then left, then right.
187	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the observation is not shape `(11,)`
188	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedPlayer/ · **Cases:** food ahead and safe; food left but danger left
189	- **Used by:** lab; PL-03, PL-04
190	
191	### baselines.RandomSwimmer: Random tank baseline
192	`class RandomSwimmer(Agent): def __init__(self, n_agents: int, *, seed: int)`
193	- **Invariants:** each agent's swim vector is drawn uniformly from the unit disc, whatever `explore` says: the random fish (BASE-04) and the random predator and prey (BASE-08). `learn` is a no-op.
194	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.RandomSwimmer/ · **Cases:** a replay double of 10 seeded `act` calls for 8 fish
195	- **Used by:** lab; PL-03, PL-04
196	
197	### baselines.ScriptedFish: Scripted tank baseline
198	`class ScriptedFish(Agent): def __init__(self) -> None`
199	- **Invariants:** BASE-05 on the full view: each fish swims at length 1 directly away from the ball's centre; with the ball on its centre, toward the tank's centre, or along `(1, 0)` at the centre. Deterministic.
200	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the observation is not `(n, 8)`
201	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedFish/ · **Cases:** ball at the origin, fish at (0.3, 0.4); ball on the fish's centre
202	- **Used by:** lab; PL-03, PL-04
203	
204	### baselines.ScriptedPredator: Scripted predator
205	`class ScriptedPredator(Agent): def __init__(self) -> None`
206	- **Invariants:** BASE-06 on `PREDATOR_FIELDS`: a length-1 vector toward the nearest visible prey, otherwise toward the tank's centre (or `(1, 0)` at the centre). Deterministic.
207	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the observation is not `(1, 19)`
208	- **Boundary:** lab, selfplay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedPredator/ · **Cases:** a prey at relative (0.3, 0.4); no prey visible at (0.5, 0)
209	- **Used by:** lab, selfplay; PL-01, PL-03, PL-04
210	
211	### baselines.ScriptedPrey: Scripted prey
212	`class ScriptedPrey(Agent): def __init__(self) -> None`
213	- **Invariants:** BASE-07 on `PREY_FIELDS`, per prey: flee at length 1 when the predator is visible and within 0.3, else head at length 1 for the nearest visible pellet, else `(0, 0)`. Deterministic.
214	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the observation is not `(n, 18)`
215	- **Boundary:** lab, selfplay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/baselines.ScriptedPrey/ · **Cases:** predator at relative (0.12, 0.16); predator out of range with a pellet at (0.3, 0)
216	- **Used by:** lab, selfplay; PL-01, PL-03, PL-04
217	
218	## agents
219	
220	### agents.TabularAgent: Table-based value learner
221	`class TabularAgent(Agent): def __init__(self, n_actions: int, params: AgentParams, exploration: Exploration, *, seed: int, explore_enabled: bool = True)`
222	- **Invariants:** single-agent (batches of 1). One value per (observation, action), keyed on the observation's bytes and starting at 0. Each `learn` moves the value toward `reward + gamma · max next value`, with no bootstrap when `terminated` (AGENT-01). `explore_enabled=False` is the `no_explore` ablation (ABL-03). The checkpoint is NPZ.
223	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agents.TabularAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of 5 act/learn calls on the corridor
224	- **Used by:** lab; PL-01, PL-03
225	
226	### agents.DQNAgent: Neural value learner
227	`class DQNAgent(Agent): def __init__(self, team: TeamSpec, params: AgentParams, exploration: Exploration, *, seed: int, ablation: Literal["none", "no_memory", "no_target"] = "none")`
228	- **Invariants:** single-agent (batches of 1), its observation shape and action count from `team` (AD-18). AGENT-03–AGENT-05 and AD-05. With `ablation="no_memory"`, each update uses only the latest transition (ABL-01). With `"no_target"`, targets come from the online network (ABL-02). No updates happen before `learn_start` transitions. The checkpoint is a PyTorch state dict.
229	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `team` has more than one agent or continuous actions, or if `net="cnn"` and `team.obs_shape` is not 3-D
230	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agents.DQNAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of 5 act/learn calls on the compact view after `learn_start`
231	- **Used by:** lab; PL-01, PL-03
232	
233	### agents.ReinforceAgent: Plain policy-gradient learner
234	`class ReinforceAgent(Agent): def __init__(self, obs_shape: tuple[int, ...], actions: ActionSpace, params: AgentParams, *, seed: int)`
235	- **Invariants:** PG-01, PG-02, AD-11. Collects whole games, and after `games_per_update` of them takes one gradient step on the return-to-go, normalised within the batch. `act(explore=True)` samples the Gaussian; `explore=False` returns its mean. The checkpoint is a PyTorch state dict.
236	- **Errors:** `ValueError` unless `actions.kind == "continuous"`
237	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agents.ReinforceAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of one 3-game update on one fish
238	- **Used by:** lab; PL-01, PL-03
239	
240	### agents.PPOAgent: Clipped policy-gradient learner
241	`class PPOAgent(Agent): def __init__(self, obs_shape: tuple[int, ...], actions: ActionSpace, params: AgentParams, *, seed: int)`
242	- **Invariants:** PG-01, PG-03, PG-04, AD-11. One shared policy and value network acts for every active agent. After `rollout_steps` agent-steps it computes advantages (GAE with `gae_lambda`), then runs `epochs` passes of minibatches of `batch_size` on the clipped objective. Inactive agents' steps never enter a batch. The checkpoint is a PyTorch state dict.
243	- **Errors:** `ValueError` unless `actions.kind == "continuous"`
244	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agents.PPOAgent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of one 64-step rollout and update on 8 fish
245	- **Used by:** lab; PL-01, PL-03
246	
247	### agents.returns_to_go: Discounted returns-to-go
248	`def returns_to_go(rewards: np.ndarray, gamma: float) -> np.ndarray`
249	- **Invariants:** `G_t = r_t + gamma · G_{t+1}` within one game, with `G` after the last step 0; output has the input's length, float64 (PG-02).
250	- **Errors:** `ValueError` unless `0 < gamma ≤ 1` and `rewards` is 1-D
251	- **Boundary:** agents · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agents.returns_to_go/ · **Cases:** a 3-step game
252	- **Used by:** agents (ReinforceAgent); PL-01
253	
254	### agents.gae: Generalised advantage estimates
255	`def gae(rewards: np.ndarray, values: np.ndarray, terminated: np.ndarray, gamma: float, lam: float) -> np.ndarray`
256	- **Invariants:** `δ_t = r_t + gamma · V(s_{t+1}) · (1 − terminated_t) − V(s_t)`, `A_t = δ_t + gamma · lam · (1 − terminated_t) · A_{t+1}`; `values` has one more entry than `rewards` (the bootstrap value after the last step); output float64 of the rewards' length (PG-03).
257	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if the lengths don't match or `lam` is outside `[0, 1]`
258	- **Boundary:** agents · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agents.gae/ · **Cases:** a 2-step rollout ending in a catch; a 3-step truncated rollout
259	- **Used by:** agents (PPOAgent); PL-01
260	
261	### agents.clipped_objective: Clipped surrogate objective
262	`def clipped_objective(ratio: np.ndarray, advantage: np.ndarray, clip: float) -> np.ndarray`
263	- **Invariants:** elementwise `min(ratio · advantage, clip(ratio, 1 − clip, 1 + clip) · advantage)`, the quantity PPO maximises (PG-03); works on NumPy arrays, and the agent applies the same formula to tensors.
264	- **Errors:** `ValueError` unless `0 < clip < 1` and the shapes match
265	- **Boundary:** agents · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/agents.clipped_objective/ · **Cases:** four (ratio, advantage) pairs straddling the clip
266	- **Used by:** agents (PPOAgent); PL-01
267	
268	## runs
269	
270	### runs.CurvePoint: type
271	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class CurvePoint: step: int; games: int; mean_score: float; elapsed_s: float`
272	- **Invariants:** `step` strictly increases within a run. `mean_score` comes from a greedy evaluation of `eval_games` games.
273	- **Boundary:** train, reference, viz · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/runs.CurvePoint/ · **Cases:** the first point at step 0; a mid-run point
274	- **Used by:** train, reference, viz; PL-01, PL-02, PL-04
275	
276	### runs.GameRecording: type
277	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class GameRecording: seed: int; board: Literal["grid", "tank"]; frames: np.ndarray; rewards: np.ndarray; scores: dict[str, np.ndarray]`
278	- **Invariants:** `frames` holds the start frame plus one per step: T×H×W int8 for `grid`, T×K×4 float32 circles for `tank` (AD-12). `rewards` is the per-step total over all agents, length T−1; `scores` maps each team to its score at every frame, length T.
279	- **Boundary:** evaluate, viz · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/runs.GameRecording/ · **Cases:** a 12-step Snake game ending in a self-collision; a 9-step corridor game; a 30-step 8-fish tank game with 2 catches; a 40-step predator-prey game with 1 catch
280	- **Used by:** evaluate, viz, lab; PL-01, PL-03, PL-05
281	
282	### runs.RunRecord: type
283	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class RunRecord: path: Path; settings: RunSettings; curve: tuple[CurvePoint, ...]; status: Literal["running", "complete", "incomplete"]; checkpoints: dict[str, Path]; generations: dict[str, tuple[Path, ...]]; games: tuple[GameRecording, ...]`
284	- **Invariants:** `settings` round-trips through `settings.json` unchanged. `checkpoints` holds one final checkpoint per learning team (single-team runs: `SOLO`), present when `status` is `complete`. `generations` holds each learning team's numbered generations in order (10 when `settings.generations == 10`, else empty) (RUN-02, SELF-04).
285	- **Boundary:** train, reference, evaluate, viz, lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/runs.RunRecord/ · **Cases:** a complete tabular corridor run; an incomplete Snake run with 3 curve points; a complete predator-prey rung-3 run with 10 generations per team
286	- **Used by:** train, reference, evaluate, viz, lab, selfplay; PL-01, PL-02, PL-03, PL-04, PL-05, PL-06
287	
288	### runs.RunWriter: Run folder writer
289	```python
290	class RunWriter:
291	    def add_point(self, p: CurvePoint) -> None: ...
292	    def save_checkpoint(self, agent: Agent, *, team: str = SOLO) -> None: ...
293	    def save_generation(self, agent: Agent, *, team: str, index: int) -> Path: ...
294	    def save_games(self, games: Sequence[GameRecording]) -> None: ...
295	    def finish(self, status: Literal["complete", "incomplete"]) -> RunRecord: ...
296	```
297	- **Invariants:** each `add_point` is flushed to `curve.csv` at once, so an interrupt keeps the curve. Generations are written as `generations/<team>/<index>` and returned, so a pool can load them during the run. The folder layout follows AD-06.
298	- **Boundary:** train, reference · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/runs.RunWriter/ · **Cases:** a replay double recording a 3-point run that finishes complete
299	- **Used by:** train, reference; PL-01, PL-02
300	
301	### runs.create_run: Start a run folder
302	`def create_run(settings: RunSettings, *, root: Path = Path("runs")) -> RunWriter`
303	- **Invariants:** writes `settings.json` and `status=running` before returning. The folder name is unique.
304	- **Boundary:** train, reference · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/runs.create_run/ · **Cases:** a fresh corridor run folder
305	- **Used by:** train, reference; PL-01, PL-02
306	
307	### runs.open_run: Read a run folder
308	`def open_run(path: Path) -> RunRecord`
309	- **Errors:** `FileNotFoundError` naming the run if the folder or `settings.json` is missing. `ValueError` naming the file if a recording is unreadable.
310	- **Boundary:** evaluate, viz, lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/runs.open_run/ · **Cases:** a complete corridor run; a folder missing `settings.json`
311	- **Used by:** evaluate, viz, lab; PL-03, PL-04, PL-05, PL-06
312	
313	## evaluate
314	
315	### evaluate.ScoreSet: type
316	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class ScoreSet: player: str; train_seed: int; scores: np.ndarray; games: tuple[GameRecording, ...]`
317	- **Invariants:** `scores` has one float score per evaluation game (food eaten, or mean survival steps per fish). `games` holds the first `record` games played.
318	- **Boundary:** compare, lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/evaluate.ScoreSet/ · **Cases:** 200 scripted-player scores for seed 0; 200 random-player scores for seed 0; 200 scripted-fish scores for seed 0
319	- **Used by:** compare, train, lab; PL-03, PL-04
320	
321	### evaluate.eval_seeds: Held-out evaluation seeds
322	`def eval_seeds(train_seed: int, n: int) -> list[int]`
323	- **Invariants:** returns `1_000_000 + 1000·train_seed + i` for `i < n`, never overlapping any training seed (DATA-01).
324	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if `n` > 1000 or `train_seed` ≥ 1,000,000
325	- **Boundary:** train · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/evaluate.eval_seeds/ · **Cases:** seeds for train seed 0, n=200
326	- **Used by:** evaluate, train; PL-01, PL-03
327	
328	### evaluate.EvalOptions: type
329	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class EvalOptions: team: str = SOLO; n_games: int = 200; record: int = 0`
330	- **Invariants:** `n_games` ≥ 1 and ≤ 1000 (`eval_seeds`' limit); 0 ≤ `record` ≤ `n_games`. The defaults are a single-team 200-game evaluation recording nothing (AD-17).
331	- **Boundary:** train, reference, lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/evaluate.EvalOptions/ · **Cases:** the defaults; predator team, 50 games, 5 recorded
332	- **Used by:** evaluate, train, reference, lab; PL-01, PL-02, PL-03, PL-04, PL-05
333	
334	### evaluate.evaluate: Greedy evaluation
335	`def evaluate(make_env: Callable[[], Env], agents: Mapping[str, Agent], *, player: str, train_seed: int, options: EvalOptions = EvalOptions()) -> ScoreSet`
336	- **Invariants:** `agents` has one agent per team of the environment; the scores are `options.team`'s `score:<team>`. Plays `options.n_games` games on `eval_seeds(train_seed, options.n_games)` with `explore=False`. It never calls `learn`. It records the first `options.record` games (REPLAY-03). 200 games of any environment take ≤ 60 s on the laptop (QR-04).
337	- **Boundary:** train, reference, lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/evaluate.evaluate/ · **Cases:** a fake returning the scripted-player ScoreSet for seed 0
338	- **Used by:** train, reference, lab; PL-01, PL-02, PL-03, PL-04, PL-05
339	
340	## train
341	
342	### train.train: Training loop
343	`def train(make_env: Callable[[], Env], agents: Mapping[str, Agent], settings: RunSettings, *, root: Path = Path("runs")) -> RunRecord`
344	- **Invariants:** `agents` has one agent per team; the teams `settings.opponents` marks `learn` (or `SOLO` in a single-team run) are learners. Steps the environment for `settings.steps` steps, building one batch-first `Transition` per learning team and calling that team's `learn` after every step (CORE-07). With `settings.generations`, it saves each learner's generation every tenth of the run (SELF-04); with `self_play == "pool"`, each game's opponent for every learner comes from its `selfplay.OpponentPool` (SELF-03, AD-14). Curve points evaluate the first learning team against the run's fixed opposition. It evaluates every `eval_every` steps (including step 0) into a `CurvePoint`. It prints a progress line at least every 30 s (RUN-04). It saves each learner's checkpoint and 5 recorded evaluation games at the end. The same settings give the same curve (RUN-03). It knows nothing environment-specific (CORE-02).
345	- **Errors:** on `KeyboardInterrupt`, finishes the run `incomplete`, then re-raises
346	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/train.train/ · **Cases:** a fake returning a complete 3-point corridor RunRecord
347	- **Used by:** lab; PL-01
348	
349	## reference
350	
351	### reference.train_reference: Library agent training
352	`def train_reference(settings: RunSettings, *, root: Path = Path("runs")) -> RunRecord`
353	- **Invariants:** `settings.agent == "reference"`. Trains Stable-Baselines3 DQN on Snake (AD-04), or PPO on the single-fish tank or on the predator-prey predator against scripted prey (AD-10, AD-15), through the AD-03 adapter with `device="cpu"` and the matching one-to-one settings mapping. Curve points use `evaluate.evaluate` at the same `eval_every`. The checkpoint loads into `ReferencePlayer`.
354	- **Errors:** `ValueError` unless the settings are Snake with the compact view, the tank with one fish and the full view, or predator-prey with `opponents == {"predator": "learn", "prey": "scripted"}`
355	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/reference.train_reference/ · **Cases:** a fake returning a complete 3-point reference RunRecord
356	- **Used by:** lab; PL-02
357	
358	### reference.ReferencePlayer: Trained library agent as an Agent
359	`class ReferencePlayer(Agent): def __init__(self, checkpoint: Path)`
360	- **Invariants:** `act` returns the library model's deterministic action (greedy for DQN, the mean for PPO), batch-first with one agent. `learn` is a no-op.
361	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/reference.ReferencePlayer/ · **Cases:** a replay double of 5 greedy `act` calls
362	- **Used by:** lab; PL-03, PL-04
363	
364	## selfplay
365	
366	### selfplay.OpponentPool: type
367	```python
368	class OpponentPool:
369	    def __init__(self, team: str, latest: Agent, load: Callable[[Path], Agent], *, latest_share: float, seed: int) -> None: ...
370	    def add(self, generation: Path) -> None: ...
371	    def sample(self) -> Agent: ...
372	```
373	- **Invariants:** SELF-03: `sample` returns `latest` with probability `latest_share`, otherwise a uniformly chosen added generation loaded through `load`; before any `add`, always `latest`. Seeded and deterministic. Loaded generations are cached and never learn.
374	- **Boundary:** train · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/selfplay.OpponentPool/ · **Cases:** a replay double sampling 20 opponents from a pool of 3 generations
375	- **Used by:** train; PL-01
376	
377	### selfplay.CrossPlay: type
378	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class CrossPlay: run: Path; predator: np.ndarray; prey: np.ndarray; games: int`
379	- **Invariants:** `predator[i, j]` and `prey[i, j]` are the mean scores of predator generation `i` against prey generation `j` over `games` held-out games; both are 10 × 10 for a co-learning run (SELF-05).
380	- **Boundary:** viz, lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/selfplay.CrossPlay/ · **Cases:** a 3 × 3 table from a short run; the 10 × 10 shape
381	- **Used by:** viz, lab; PL-06
382	
383	### selfplay.Ratings: type
384	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Ratings: predator: np.ndarray; prey: np.ndarray`
385	- **Invariants:** SELF-06: `predator[i]` is the mean of row `i` of `CrossPlay.predator`, `prey[j]` the mean of column `j` of `CrossPlay.prey`.
386	- **Boundary:** viz, lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/selfplay.Ratings/ · **Cases:** ratings of the 3 × 3 sample
387	- **Used by:** viz, lab; PL-06
388	
389	### selfplay.cross_play: Cross-play table
390	`def cross_play(run: RunRecord, make_env: Callable[[], Env], load: Callable[[str, Path], Agent], *, games: int = 50) -> CrossPlay`
391	- **Invariants:** plays every predator generation against every prey generation of `run` with `explore=False` on `eval_seeds`, never calling `learn` (SELF-05).
392	- **Errors:** `ValueError` naming the run and the generation if one is missing
393	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/selfplay.cross_play/ · **Cases:** a fake returning the 3 × 3 CrossPlay
394	- **Used by:** lab; PL-06
395	
396	### selfplay.ratings: Generation ratings
397	`def ratings(table: CrossPlay) -> Ratings`
398	- **Invariants:** SELF-06; pure arithmetic over the table.
399	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/selfplay.ratings/ · **Cases:** the 3 × 3 sample
400	- **Used by:** lab; PL-06
401	
402	## compare
403	
404	### compare.ClaimSpec: type
405	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class ClaimSpec: name: str; a: str; b: str; direction: Literal["greater", "not_worse"]; stretch: bool = False; min_ratio: float | None = None`
406	- **Invariants:** `a` and `b` are player names present in the score input. `min_ratio` (REF-02's 0.9) checks `mean(a) ≥ min_ratio · mean(b)` on top of the test.
407	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/compare.ClaimSpec/ · **Cases:** "dqn-compact greater than random"; "dqn-compact not worse than reference with min_ratio 0.9"; the stretch claim for dqn-grid
408	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
409	
410	### compare.Claim: type
411	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Claim: spec: ClaimSpec; mean_a: float; mean_b: float; p_value: float; p_corrected: float; passed: bool; method: str`
412	- **Invariants:** `method` names what the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill chose (statistic, null distribution, correction). For `greater`, `passed` means significant at 0.05 after correction. For `not_worse`, it means "a worse than b" is not significant, and `min_ratio` holds when set.
413	- **Boundary:** lab, viz · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/compare.Claim/ · **Cases:** a passed "greater" claim; a failed "not_worse" claim; a stretch claim that failed
414	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
415	
416	### compare.run_claims: Tested claims and report
417	`def run_claims(scores: Mapping[str, Sequence[ScoreSet]], specs: Sequence[ClaimSpec], *, alpha: float = 0.05, charts: Sequence[Path] = (), out: Path) -> list[Claim]`
418	- **Invariants:** each player's scores are pooled across its 5 training seeds. The correction covers every spec in one call. Writes `out/report.md` listing every player's mean and spread (BASE-03) and every claim, with stretch claims labelled "stretch" (EVAL-05), and links each chart image in `charts` by its path under a "Cross-play" section (EVAL-11). `compare` never imports `viz` (AD-18).
419	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if a spec names a player missing from `scores`
420	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/compare.run_claims/ · **Cases:** a fake returning one passed and one failed Claim
421	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
422	
423	## viz
424	
425	### viz.Chart: type
426	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Chart: path: Path; groups: tuple[str, ...]; baselines: tuple[str, ...]`
427	- **Invariants:** `path` is a written PNG. `groups` and `baselines` list what the chart draws, in legend order.
428	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/viz.Chart/ · **Cases:** the ladder chart (three agents and the reference, two baselines); the ablation chart
429	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
430	
431	### viz.plot_curves: Learning-curve chart
432	`def plot_curves(groups: Mapping[str, Sequence[RunRecord]], *, scores: Mapping[str, Sequence[ScoreSet]], baselines: Sequence[str], out: Path) -> Chart`
433	- **Invariants:** one line per group (the mean over its seeds, with a min–max band). Each name in `baselines` gets a horizontal line at its pooled mean from `scores` (EVAL-06). Several groups on one chart form the ablation comparison (ABL-04).
434	- **Errors:** `ValueError` if a baseline name is missing from `scores`
435	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/viz.plot_curves/ · **Cases:** a fake returning the ladder Chart
436	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
437	
438	### viz.plot_crossplay: Cross-play heat map and ratings
439	`def plot_crossplay(table: CrossPlay, ratings: Ratings, *, title: str, label: str = "", out: Path) -> Chart`
440	- **Invariants:** one heat map per team (predator generation by prey generation) and a line chart of both teams' ratings by generation; `label` (such as "reported") is printed on the figure (EVAL-11).
441	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/viz.plot_crossplay/ · **Cases:** a fake returning the rung-2 Chart
442	- **Used by:** lab; PL-06
443	
444	### viz.Replay: type
445	```python
446	class Replay:
447	    def __init__(self, game: GameRecording, *, fps: float = 8.0) -> None: ...
448	    def advance(self) -> int: ...
449	    def show(self) -> None: ...
450	```
451	- **Invariants:** each frame shows the board (the grid; or the tank with the ball, the fish, the predator, the prey and the pellets, caught animals greyed), the scores and the step (REPLAY-01). Space toggles pause, the right arrow calls `advance` once, and `+`/`-` double or halve `fps` (REPLAY-02). `advance` returns the new frame index and stops at the last frame.
452	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/viz.Replay/ · **Cases:** a replay double advancing a 12-frame game to its end
453	- **Used by:** lab; PL-05
454	
455	## lab
456	
457	### lab.PlayerTarget: type
458	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class PlayerTarget: runs: tuple[Path, ...] = (); baseline: str | None = None; team: str = SOLO; vs: tuple[str, ...] = ()`
459	- **Invariants:** exactly one of `runs` (the player's run folders, one per training seed) and `baseline` (`random` or `scripted`) is set. `team` and `vs` matter only for predator-prey, where `vs` lists the panel opponents (SELF-07).
460	- **Boundary:** lab.players · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/lab.PlayerTarget/ · **Cases:** dqn-compact over 5 run folders; the scripted baseline; a predator-prey prey player against the scripted and random predators
461	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
462	
463	### lab.ReportTargets: type
464	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class ReportTargets: players: Mapping[str, PlayerTarget]; baselines: tuple[str, ...]; ladder: tuple[str, ...]; ablations: tuple[str, ...]; crossplay: tuple[Path, ...] = ()`
465	- **Invariants:** `baselines`, `ladder` and `ablations` name players in `players`, in legend order; `crossplay` holds PL-06's chart images. Read from `report.toml` with the `ClaimSpec`s.
466	- **Boundary:** lab.players · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/lab.ReportTargets/ · **Cases:** M1's Snake report (four ladder players, three ablations, two baselines, no cross-play)
467	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
468	
469	### lab.CurveRuns: type
470	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class CurveRuns: ladder: Mapping[str, Sequence[RunRecord]]; ablations: Mapping[str, Sequence[RunRecord]]`
471	- **Invariants:** keyed by player name in `ReportTargets` order, one `RunRecord` per training seed; each map is one `viz.plot_curves` call's `groups`.
472	- **Boundary:** lab · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/lab.CurveRuns/ · **Cases:** the M1 ladder and ablation groups over 5 seeds of 3-point runs
473	- **Used by:** lab; PL-04
474	
475	### lab.main: Command line
476	`def main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> int`
477	- **Invariants:** subcommands are `train <settings.toml> [--seeds 0-4]`, `eval <run | random | scripted>… [--env snake|tank|predprey] [--team T]`, `replay <run> [--game N | --new --seed S | --pair <predator gen> <prey gen>]`, `crossplay <run>` and `report <report.toml>`. `train` builds the env and agent from the settings alone (RUN-01), and runs reference settings through `train_reference`. An ablation settings file carries its full agent's step budget, and `report` refuses an ablation whose seeds or steps differ from its full agent's (ABL-04). Returns 0 on success, 2 on bad input.
478	- **Errors:** bad settings and missing runs print the `ValueError` or `FileNotFoundError` message and return 2
479	- **Used by:** the learner; PL-01, PL-02, PL-03, PL-04, PL-05, PL-06

/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/build-planning/references/phase0.md-25-
/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/build-planning/references/phase0.md-26-**b. Step spines** (`**Kind:** spine`, `**Step:** PL-NN.<function>`, solo, XS or S). One per new step, each depending only on the interface task: it replaces the stub naming it with the step's spine version from the step table. `Writes:` its owner module and that module's unit test, nothing else; steps sharing one module share one spine task. Acceptance: the step's input sample gives a schema-valid output; the signature is unchanged. A step another pipeline provides (`PL-01 as built`) gets no spine.
/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/build-planning/references/phase0.md-27-
/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/build-planning/references/phase0.md-28-**c. Wiring** (`**Kind:** wiring`, `**Step:** PL-NN`, solo). One per new pipeline: the entry function in the pipeline's own entry module (the layout's, never one module shared by several pipelines), so wiring tasks run side by side, and its smoke test. `__main__.py` is the interface task's. It depends on its pipeline's spines, since the smoke runs them (`order`, `none: integration: …`), never on their thicken tasks. A call to another pipeline or package runs against that boundary's fake, so wiring PL-02 never waits for PL-01. Acceptance: the smoke command runs end to end and is committed as the pipeline's smoke test; the pipeline's pre-build sections are replaced by a `dt layout` pointer (`dt land` runs `dt prebuild-clear` when the wiring lands).
/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/build-planning/references/phase0.md-29-
/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/build-planning/references/phase0.md-30-**d. System task** (`**Kind:** system`, solo, last). It swaps each fake for the real provider, in the entry its wiring wrote (a `file` edge), and runs every smoke command and the milestone's golden cases on the real chain; the boundary samples still validate against real output. Multi-package: one per new system pipeline, `**Step:**` that pipeline, `**Package:**` the owner of its first step, `**Reason:** system task`; the root has no pre-build sections, so no `dt layout` bullet. Single package: `**Step:**` the calling pipeline. A milestone with no fake has no system task.

/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/architecture-design/references/contracts.md-10-- **Type sections.** Every type one step hands to another, and every type a public signature takes or returns, is its own section with the title `type`: `### profile.Profile: type`. The first line is its shape as code: a frozen dataclass's fields, a `typing.Protocol`, or a pandera schema's columns for a dataframe. Then **Invariants** and **Used by**. `pipeline-design` names these types in every step's `In → Out`; the build's interface task writes them, as code, into the owner's `types.py`. Freeze only what crosses: a helper's or a private function's types stay out.
/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/architecture-design/references/contracts.md-11-- **Contract first:** the contract is defined before either side is built. **The provider owns it.**
/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/rl9-incremental-A3-r1/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/architecture-design/references/contracts.md:12:- **Boundaries.** A symbol consumed across owners adds one line: `- **Boundary:** <consumers> · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/<id>.<symbol>/ · **Cases:** <the payloads a fixture must show>`. Every type that crosses step owners has one, components included: consumers are packages, components, or both. This section is the boundary's source of truth. `build-planning`'s interface task freezes it into that folder: a schema (JSON Schema across runtimes; a pandera schema for a dataframe in Python), one sample per case, and, for a function a pipeline calls from another pipeline or package, a fixture-backed fake of the provider. A Protocol or class type's case is a replay double (a fixture-backed recording of the real object's calls, `a recorded 5-batch epoch`), and so is a function another component's step calls: it is the type's sample, not a fake. Both sides then build at once against the same samples. A type needs at least one case (`dt lint` `type-cases`). Name the cases; don't write the fixtures. A case may name a probe's redacted sample (`a posted purchase (PB-01 samples/sync_added.json#0)`); the interface task derives the type's sample from it, never copies it.

971	                covered = (_types_between(ctx, types, src, dst) if e.kind == "data"
972	                           else _callees(ctx, src, dst)) & produced
973	                if covered:
974	                    yield _p(doc, where, "edges",
975	                             f"{e.src} waits on code task {e.dst} across {', '.join(sorted(covered))}, which an "
976	                             "interface task freezes: mitigate the edge and drop the dependency")
977	            rows.setdefault((e.src, e.dst), e)
978	        for t in tasks:
979	            line = md.field_line(doc.lines, t.start, t.end, "Depends on")

137	```python
138	def run_crossplay(run: Path, label: str, out: Path) -> Chart:
139	    record = open_run(run)
140	    make = make_env(record.settings)
141	    table = cross_play(record, make, load_generation)
142	    scores = ratings(table)
143	    return plot_crossplay(table, scores, title=record.settings.name, label=label, out=out)

1	# RL Lab — Architecture
2	
3	**Status:** Approved · **Shape:** Single package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-18 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)
4	
5	## Overview
6	Every run starts from one TOML settings file. `lab` turns the settings into an environment (Snake, the corridor, the fish tank or predator-prey) and one agent per team of that environment (a value learner, a policy-gradient learner, a baseline or the library reference). `train` steps the environment, one batch-first step per team at a time, and hands each learning team's agent its steps to learn from. In predator-prey it also saves generations and, for self-play, draws each game's opponents from `selfplay`'s pools of past generations; `selfplay` later plays those generations against each other for the cross-play table. Every few thousand steps `train` asks `evaluate` for a short greedy evaluation, which becomes a point on the learning curve. A finished run leaves a folder holding its settings, curve, checkpoint and recorded games. `evaluate` scores saved runs on seeds never used in training. `compare` turns those scores into statistically tested claims, and `viz` draws the curves and replays games. All environments sit behind one `core.Env` protocol, which carries one or more teams of agents per step with discrete or continuous actions (AD-09, AD-13), so every environment shares every other component.
7	
8	```mermaid
9	flowchart LR
10	  lab --> train --> evaluate
11	  lab --> reference --> evaluate
12	  train --> runs
13	  evaluate --> runs
14	  lab --> compare --> evaluate
15	  lab --> viz --> runs
16	  train --> selfplay --> evaluate
17	  lab --> selfplay
18	  viz --> evaluate
19	  train --> core
20	  envs --> core
21	  agents --> core
22	  baselines --> envs
23	```
24	
25	## Tech stack
26	| Area | Choice | Decision |
27	|---|---|---|
28	| Language | Python 3.12 | AD-01 |
29	| Arrays | NumPy ≥ 2.0 | AD-02 |
30	| Neural networks | PyTorch ≥ 2.5, CPU only, one thread | AD-02, AD-08 |
31	| Environment interface | Own `core.Env`, shaped like Gymnasium's reset/step, batch-first over each team's agents | AD-03, AD-09, AD-13 |
32	| Reference agents | Stable-Baselines3 ≥ 2.4 DQN (Snake) and PPO (single-fish tank; predator against scripted prey), with Gymnasium ≥ 1.0 for the adapter only; optional extra `rllab[reference]` | AD-04, AD-10, AD-15 |
33	| Tank physics | Vectorised NumPy, one array per quantity over all animals; shared by the fish tank and predator-prey | AD-12, AD-16 |
34	| Settings and run records | TOML in; JSON, CSV and NPZ out, in one folder per run | AD-06 |
35	| Charts and replay | Matplotlib ≥ 3.9 | AD-07 |
36	| Tests and tooling | pytest, import-linter, uv | — |
37	
38	## Components
39	| Component | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on |
40	|---|---|---|---|
41	| `core` | `Env` and `Agent` protocols, teams and action spaces, shared step types, run settings and seeding | CORE-01, CORE-05, CORE-06, DATA-02, RUN-01, HUNT-07 | — |
42	| `envs` | Snake (compact and full-grid views), the 1-D corridor, the fish tank (full and limited views) and predator-prey, behind `core.Env` | SNAKE-01–SNAKE-09, CORE-03, FISH-01–FISH-10, HUNT-01–HUNT-06, HUNT-08, HUNT-09 | `core` |
43	| `baselines` | Random and scripted players for each environment, behind `core.Agent` | BASE-01, BASE-02, BASE-04–BASE-08 | `core`, `envs` |
44	| `agents` | Hand-written learners: table-based and neural value learners with their ablation switches, and the plain and clipped policy-gradient learners | AGENT-01–AGENT-07, ABL-01–ABL-03, PG-01–PG-04 | `core` |
45	| `runs` | Writes and reads run folders: settings, curve, checkpoints per team, generations, recorded games, status | RUN-02, RUN-05, SELF-04 | `core` |
46	| `evaluate` | Greedy evaluation on held-out seeds; records games from any checkpoint | EVAL-01, DATA-01, BASE-03, REPLAY-03, QR-04 | `core`, `runs` |
47	| `train` | Environment-agnostic training loop, learning curve and progress lines | CORE-02, CORE-07, RUN-03, RUN-04, SELF-01, SELF-02, SELF-04, QR-01, QR-02, QR-03, QR-07 | `core`, `runs`, `evaluate`, `selfplay` |
48	| `reference` | Trains the library agent on each environment's compared rung, with any other team frozen, and wraps it as a `core.Agent` | REF-01 | `core`, `envs`, `runs`, `evaluate` |
49	| `selfplay` | Opponent pools of past generations, the cross-play table and generation ratings | SELF-03, SELF-05, SELF-06 | `core`, `runs`, `evaluate` |
50	| `compare` | Runs each claim with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill and writes the report | EVAL-02–EVAL-05, EVAL-07–EVAL-10, REF-02, REF-03 | `evaluate` |
51	| `viz` | Learning-curve and ablation charts; interactive replay | EVAL-06, EVAL-11, ABL-04, REPLAY-01, REPLAY-02 | `runs`, `evaluate`, `selfplay` |
52	| `lab` | Command line: builds env and agent from settings; train, eval, replay and report commands | RUN-01, ABL-04, CORE-04, PG-05, SELF-07, REPLAY-03 | `core`, `envs`, `baselines`, `agents`, `runs`, `evaluate`, `selfplay`, `train`, `reference`, `compare`, `viz` |
53	
54	**Seams:** `reference` is the only importer of Stable-Baselines3 and Gymnasium, so it can move into its own package without touching the rest. `agents` is the only importer of PyTorch.
55	
56	**Skill routing:** `compare` owns every significance claim. It hands the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill two score sets and a direction (for example "dqn-compact greater than random", "ppo-1 greater than reinforce-1" or "rung-3 prey greater than rung-2 prey against the prey panel"). The skill chooses the test statistic, the null distribution and the multiple-comparison correction, and `compare` records the skill's verdict as a `compare.Claim`. `compare` adds no test procedure of its own. The 90%-of-mean check in REF-02 is plain arithmetic, not a test.
57	
58	## Interfaces
59	See [packages/rllab/contract.md](packages/rllab/contract.md).
60	
61	## Data
62	- All data comes from the lab's own environments (DATA-02). No downloads.
63	- **Seeds (DATA-01):** a run's training seed `s` must be below 1,000,000. Its evaluation seeds are `1_000_000 + 1000·s + i`, a disjoint range. `evaluate.eval_seeds` asserts that none of them overlaps the run's training seed stream.
64	- **Ladder runs:** 5 training seeds (0–4) for each rung. Snake: `tabular-compact`, `dqn-compact`, `dqn-grid`, `reference-compact` and the three ablations. Fish tank: `reinforce-1`, `ppo-1`, `ppo-school`, `ppo-school-limited` and `reference-ppo-1`. Predator-prey: `pp-r1-prey`, `pp-r1-predator`, `pp-r2`, `pp-r3` and `reference-pp-predator`; the panels (SELF-07) are each team's scripted and random players plus the other team's rung-1 policy. Each environment's `random` and `scripted` players are evaluated on the same evaluation seeds. Run settings live in `settings/`.
65	
66	## Cross-cutting concerns
67	- **Reproducibility (RUN-03):** one seed feeds a single `np.random.Generator` and `torch.manual_seed`, passed down explicitly. PyTorch runs with one thread and deterministic algorithms (AD-08). No global random state.
68	- **Timing (QR-01–QR-04, QR-07):** predator-prey runs count both learning teams' updates inside one run's 60 minutes. Each run's progress lines and `curve.csv` carry elapsed seconds, so the limits are read from the runs themselves. A timing test trains each agent for 10,000 steps and extrapolates to its full step budget.
69	- **Laptop only (QR-06):** PyTorch is pinned to CPU. Nothing selects a GPU. Stable-Baselines3 is given `device="cpu"`, because its default `"auto"` would otherwise pick any GPU it finds.
70	- **Cloud cap (QR-05):** a rung may go to rented hardware only after its laptop timing test fails QR-03 or QR-07. Spending is tracked by hand against $5 per stage.
71	- **From scratch (AGENT-06):** import-linter keeps `selfplay` below `train` and above `evaluate`, and forbids `agents`, `core`, `envs`, `baselines`, `selfplay` and `train` from importing `stable_baselines3` or `gymnasium`.
72	- **Errors:** bad settings raise `ValueError` naming the key. Stepping a finished game raises `RuntimeError`. A missing or broken run folder raises `FileNotFoundError` naming the run.
73	- **Testing:** pytest, with rule tests per Snake and tank requirement against hand-built states (`Snake.reset_to`, `FishTank.reset_to`, `PredatorPrey.reset_to`).
74	
75	## Project layout
76	```
77	src/rllab/   → core/ envs/ baselines/ agents/ runs/ evaluate/ selfplay/ train/ reference/ compare/ viz/ lab/ — one folder per component; __init__ exports its contract
78	settings/    → one TOML file per run family (ladder rungs, ablations, corridor, reference)
79	runs/        → run folders, git-ignored
80	reports/     → comparison reports and charts
81	tests/       → folders only; files are planned in pipelines.md
82	```
83	
84	## Decisions
85	### AD-01: Python 3.12, one package `rllab`
86	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
87	- **Context:** A solo learning lab with one install on one laptop. The brief wants a core reused across environments, not separately deployed parts.
88	- **Decision:** One Python 3.12 package, `rllab`, with one folder per component.
89	- **Alternatives:** A package per environment — no real boundary yet; the core would be guessed before the second environment exists. Python 3.13 — some scientific wheels lag.
90	- **Consequences:** Pin `requires-python = ">=3.12,<3.13"`. A later environment adds an `envs` module and a settings file, not a package. The contract runs past the 200-line guide for one package; that is accepted because tasks load only the sections they read. `reference` sits behind an optional extra, ready to split out if it ever needs its own install. It also stays one package with five or more pipelines and twelve components, at or above the ~3-pipeline and ~12-module Grow guides. All five are commands of one command line over the same run folders, and each has its own entry module, so their tasks never share a file. PyTorch stays because `agents` is its only importer.
91	
92	### AD-02: Hand-written agents on NumPy arrays and PyTorch tensors
93	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `agents`
94	- **Context:** AGENT-06 needs the agents written by hand from array and tensor operations, with automatic gradients allowed. The lesson is value learning, not backpropagation.
95	- **Decision:** The table-based agent is pure NumPy. The neural agents use PyTorch tensors, `torch.nn` layers, autograd and the Adam optimizer. Everything RL-specific is written by hand in `agents`: the memory of past steps, sampling, the target copy, the learning targets, the loss and the exploration schedule.
96	- **Alternatives:** NumPy only, with hand-written backpropagation — more to learn, but debugging gradients would crowd out the RL lesson, and the CNN in AD-05 becomes a project of its own. JAX — capable, but less familiar CPU tooling on a Mac.
97	- **Consequences:** PyTorch is a dependency of `agents` only. Swapping in hand-written backpropagation later stays local to the network module.
98	
99	### AD-03: Own environment protocol, shaped like Gymnasium
100	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `core`
101	- **Context:** CORE-01 needs one interface for every environment. REF-01 needs the library agent to train on the same Snake. AGENT-06 keeps library code out of the lab's agents.
102	- **Decision:** `core.Env` is a small Protocol whose `reset(seed)` returns `(obs, info)` and whose `step(actions)` returns a `StepResult` with Gymnasium's fields (observation, reward, terminated, truncated, info), plus `frame()` for replay. AD-09 sets their batch-first shape over agents. `reference` wraps one single-agent team of a `core.Env`, with every other team played by a frozen agent (AD-15), in a `gymnasium.Env` adapter.
103	- **Alternatives:** Subclass `gymnasium.Env` directly — no adapter, but every environment and the core then depend on Gymnasium. A bespoke interface unlike Gymnasium's — needs a bigger adapter and teaches a nonstandard shape.
104	- **Consequences:** Truncation (starvation or the step cap) and termination (a collision or a catch) stay separate, so learning targets bootstrap correctly on truncation. The adapter is checked with Stable-Baselines3's `check_env`.
105	
106	### AD-04: Stable-Baselines3 DQN as the reference implementation
107	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `reference`
108	- **Context:** For Snake, REF-01–REF-03 need an established implementation trained with the same view, rewards, step budget and seeds as the compact-view neural agent; AD-10 covers the tank. The Stable-Baselines3 DQN docs (https://stable-baselines3.readthedocs.io/en/master/modules/dqn.html, read 2026-10-17) say it supports Discrete actions and Box observations, has `buffer_size`, `target_update_interval`, `exploration_fraction`, `seed` and `device`, and offers `MlpPolicy` for vector observations. The custom-environment guide (https://stable-baselines3.readthedocs.io/en/master/guide/custom_env.html, read 2026-10-17) requires a `gymnasium.Env` with `reset(seed, options) → (obs, info)` and a five-field `step`, checked by `check_env`.
109	- **Decision:** `reference` trains Stable-Baselines3 DQN with `MlpPolicy` on the compact view through the AD-03 adapter, with `device="cpu"`. Its hidden layers, learning rate, discount, batch size, memory size, target interval and exploration schedule are mapped one-to-one from the same `RunSettings` the hand-written agent uses. Its curve points come from `evaluate`, exactly as the lab's own agents' do.
110	- **Alternatives:** CleanRL's single-file DQN — very readable, but a script to copy rather than a library to call, so "matching" it blurs into copying it. RLlib — far heavier than one laptop needs.
111	- **Consequences:** Stable-Baselines3 measures exploration as a fraction of training rather than in decay steps, and updates every `train_freq` steps. The mapping fixes `train_freq=1` and `gradient_steps=1`, and sets `exploration_fraction = decay_steps / steps`. Stable-Baselines3 and Gymnasium are an optional install extra, so the rest of the lab never carries them.
112	- **Probe:** none — the two pages above settle the spaces, parameters and environment interface. No account or plan is involved.
113	
114	### AD-05: An MLP for the compact view, a small CNN for the full grid
115	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `agents`
116	- **Context:** AGENT-03 takes an 11-value compact view. AGENT-05 takes the full grid. QR-02 and QR-03 give each 60 CPU minutes.
117	- **Decision:** Compact view: two hidden layers of 128 units, the same shape the reference uses. Full grid: two 3×3 convolution layers (16 and 32 channels) and one 128-unit hidden layer, over a 3×10×10 head/body/food grid.
118	- **Alternatives:** An MLP on the flattened grid — cheaper per step but slower to learn spatial patterns. It stays the fallback if the CNN misses QR-03.
119	- **Consequences:** The network is chosen in settings (`net = "mlp"` or `"cnn"`), so the fallback is a settings change, not a code change.
120	
121	### AD-06: One folder per run; TOML settings in, plain files out
122	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `runs`
123	- **Context:** RUN-01 needs a run fully described by its settings. RUN-02 needs settings, seed, curve, checkpoint and games saved. RUN-05 needs reloading. The interrupted-run edge case needs partial records kept.
124	- **Decision:** Settings are read from TOML into a frozen `core.RunSettings`. Each run writes `runs/<name>-s<seed>-<timestamp>/` holding `settings.json` (the resolved settings), `curve.csv`, `checkpoint.pt` or `checkpoint.npz`, `games.npz` and `status` (`running`, `complete` or `incomplete`). The curve is appended as the run goes. An interrupt marks the run `incomplete`.
125	- **Alternatives:** An experiment tracker such as MLflow or W&B — a server or account for a one-person lab. One SQLite file — harder to inspect by eye and to delete one run from.
126	- **Consequences:** A run is inspectable with any text editor and deleted by removing its folder. Nothing indexes runs, so reports name their run folders explicitly.
127	
128	### AD-07: Matplotlib for charts and the replay viewer
129	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `viz`
130	- **Context:** EVAL-06 and ABL-04 need charts. REPLAY-01 and REPLAY-02 need a plain player with pause, step and speed control. The brief rules out polished graphics.
131	- **Decision:** Matplotlib draws the curves and plays recorded frames in an interactive window, as grid cells or tank circles by the recording's board kind (AD-12). Space pauses, the right arrow steps one frame, and `+`/`-` change speed.
132	- **Alternatives:** pygame — smoother, but a second graphics dependency. A terminal viewer (curses) — no dependency, but charts would still need Matplotlib.
133	- **Consequences:** One plotting dependency. Replay needs a GUI backend, which macOS has by default. Tests drive the viewer headless through its frame-advance function.
134	
135	### AD-08: Single-threaded, deterministic CPU training
136	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
137	- **Context:** RUN-03 needs identical curves from identical settings and seed on one machine. QR-06 rules out NVIDIA GPUs. The networks are small.
138	- **Decision:** `torch.set_num_threads(1)`, `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`, CPU device everywhere. The Apple GPU backend is not used.
139	- **Alternatives:** The Apple GPU backend — no gain on networks this small, and its determinism is weaker. Several threads — little gain, and reduction order can vary.
140	- **Consequences:** The 5 seeds of one ladder rung can run as 5 parallel processes without fighting over threads.
141	
142	### AD-09: A batch-first environment interface over agents, adopted before M1 is built
143	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `core`
144	- **Context:** CORE-01 now needs one interface for Snake (one agent, 3 discrete actions) and the fish tank (up to 16 fish, 2-D continuous actions). CORE-06 needs Snake and the corridor to behave as before. M1's plan is approved but no code exists yet.
145	- **Decision:** Every environment is batch-first over its agents. Observations are `(n_agents, *obs_shape)`, actions `(n_agents,)` discrete or `(n_agents, dim)` continuous, per `core.ActionSpace`. Rewards and per-agent `done` are `(n_agents,)`, and the game-level `terminated`/`truncated` stay booleans. Snake and the corridor are the `n_agents == 1` case. `core.Agent.act` takes and returns batches, and `core.Transition` carries per-agent arrays with an `active` mask. M1 adopts this interface before it is built: a Replan of its interface task, so Snake is never written against the old shape.
146	- **Alternatives:** A separate multi-agent protocol beside the single-agent one — two interfaces for `train`, `evaluate` and `replay` to handle, against CORE-07. Per-agent dicts (PettingZoo style) — flexible, but slow for 8 fish in NumPy and unneeded with a fixed agent count. Migrating after M1 is built — the honest "revise the core" lesson, at the cost of rewriting a finished milestone's core and agents.
147	- **Consequences:** AD-13 adds teams on top: each team is batch-first as above. Value learners and Snake baselines handle a batch of 1. CORE-06 is met by M1's golden cases passing on the new interface, with their expected values unchanged. `pipeline-design`, `test-planning` and `build-planning` revisit M1's types and plan before the build starts.
148	
149	### AD-10: Stable-Baselines3 PPO as the tank's reference
150	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `reference`
151	- **Context:** REF-01–REF-03 need an established implementation on the tank's compared rung, one fish with the full view. The Stable-Baselines3 PPO docs (https://stable-baselines3.readthedocs.io/en/master/modules/ppo.html, read 2026-10-17) list Box action support, the defaults `learning_rate=3e-4`, `n_steps=2048`, `batch_size=64`, `n_epochs=10`, `gamma=0.99`, `gae_lambda=0.95`, `clip_range=0.2`, `ent_coef=0.0`, `vf_coef=0.5`, `max_grad_norm=0.5`, `normalize_advantage=True`, a Tanh activation for `MlpPolicy`, and `seed` and `device`. The page doesn't state the default layer sizes.
152	- **Decision:** `reference` trains PPO with `MlpPolicy` on the single-fish tank through the AD-03 adapter (Box observation `(8,)`, Box action `(2,)` in [−1, 1]), with `device="cpu"`. `net_arch` is set explicitly to two 64-unit layers for the policy and the value function. Every other parameter maps one-to-one from the same `RunSettings` the hand-written PPO uses (AD-11).
153	- **Alternatives:** CleanRL's PPO — readable, but copied rather than called, as in AD-04. A multi-agent library for the school — out of scope (the spec's library comparison is single-fish only).
154	- **Consequences:** Setting `net_arch` explicitly removes the one default the docs leave open. The mapping test checks the built policy's layer sizes and log-std form against AD-11.
155	- **Probe:** none — the PPO page settles the action space and every parameter the mapping uses; the layer sizes are set, not read.
156	
157	### AD-11: Policy-gradient agents: a Gaussian MLP policy with the library's defaults
158	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `agents`
159	- **Context:** PG-01–PG-04 need hand-written plain and clipped policy-gradient learners for 2-D continuous actions, and REF-02 needs the clipped one to come close to the library's PPO. QR-07 gives each rung 60 CPU minutes.
160	- **Decision:** Both agents use a policy network of two 64-unit Tanh layers producing the Gaussian mean, with a state-independent learned log-std starting at 0. PPO adds a separate value network of the same shape, GAE advantages normalised per minibatch, the clipped objective, value-loss weight 0.5, no entropy bonus and gradient clipping at 0.5, all matching the AD-10 defaults. REINFORCE uses the same policy network on returns-to-go over `games_per_update` games. Actions are sampled unclipped; the tank shortens them (FISH-02). A school shares one agent instance (PG-04).
161	- **Alternatives:** A tanh-squashed Gaussian — bounded actions, but the log-probability correction adds a second thing to debug, and the library's default doesn't squash. A shared policy/value trunk — fewer parameters, but it differs from the reference.
162	- **Consequences:** The hand-written PPO and the reference differ only in code, not settings, so a REF-02 gap points at the learner's code. REINFORCE has no value baseline beyond batch normalisation, so its high variance stays visible (EVAL-08).
163	
164	### AD-12: Vectorised tank physics; recordings carry the board kind
165	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `envs`
166	- **Context:** FISH-01–FISH-10 need reproducible continuous physics for up to 16 fish within QR-04's evaluation budget. REPLAY-01 needs the tank replayed, and the grid-only recording can't hold it.
167	- **Decision:** The tank keeps NumPy arrays over all fish (positions, velocities, alive mask) and steps them in one vectorised update, with one seeded `np.random.Generator`. Its `frame()` is a `(K, 4)` float32 array of circles `(x, y, radius, kind)`: the ball, swimming fish and caught fish. `runs.GameRecording` gains `board` (`grid` or `tank`), and `viz.Replay` draws cells or circles accordingly (AD-07's viewer, same controls).
168	- **Alternatives:** A physics library — collision solving the spec rules out (FISH-07). Rasterising the tank to a grid for replay — loses the continuous positions the replay should show.
169	- **Consequences:** Ball reflection is computed exactly at the wall each step, never by small sub-steps. Recordings stay small (K ≤ 17 circles per frame).
170	
171	### AD-13: Teams in the core, adopted before M1 is built
172	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `core`
173	- **Context:** CORE-01 now needs environments with two teams of different sizes and views: predator-prey's 1-agent predator (19 values) and 6-agent prey (18 values). AD-09's batch-first arrays assume one observation shape per environment. M1 is approved but not yet built.
174	- **Decision:** `core.Env` declares `teams: Mapping[str, TeamSpec]`. Observations, actions, rewards and `done` are maps from team name to AD-09's batch-first arrays. Single-team environments use one team, `core.SOLO` (`"agents"`). One `core.Agent` acts for one team, and `train` and `evaluate` take one agent per team. `info` carries `score:<team>` per team, plus `score` for the first. M1 adopts this before it's built: a Replan of its interface task.
175	- **Alternatives:** A separate two-team protocol beside `core.Env` — two code paths in `train`, `evaluate` and replay, against CORE-07. Padding both teams to one observation shape with a team flag — one array, but a policy would see the other team's padding, and the scripted players get fiddly. Adding teams after M1 — rewrites M1's loop and evaluation.
176	- **Consequences:** M1's Snake and corridor code addresses `SOLO`; its golden values don't change. M2's fish tank is also one team. S3 adds no second loop.
177	
178	### AD-14: Self-play through opponent pools inside the shared training loop
179	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `selfplay`, `train`
180	- **Context:** SELF-01–SELF-04 need co-learning and pool-based self-play. CORE-07 forbids a training loop of predator-prey's own. SELF-05–SELF-06 need cross-play and ratings over saved generations.
181	- **Decision:** A new `selfplay` component owns `OpponentPool` (the latest policy with probability `pool_latest`, otherwise a uniformly drawn past generation, loaded once and frozen), `cross_play` and `ratings`. `train` saves each learner's generation every tenth of the run through `runs.RunWriter.save_generation`, adds it to the other team's pool, and asks the pool for each game's opponent. Both teams learn in the same loop with their own PPO agents.
182	- **Alternatives:** A separate self-play trainer — duplicates the loop, and the spec forbids it. Opponent sampling per step instead of per game — opponents would switch mid-chase, which is unrealistic and noisier.
183	- **Consequences:** `train` gains generation and pool handling, guarded by settings, so single-team runs are unaffected. Cross-play costs 100 cells × 50 games of up to 1,000 steps. It's a separate command, not part of training time.
184	
185	### AD-15: The predator-prey reference plays the predator against frozen scripted prey
186	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `reference`
187	- **Context:** REF-01–REF-03 compare rung 1's predator, trained against scripted prey, with a library PPO. The library trains one agent in a single-agent environment (AD-10's docs).
188	- **Decision:** The AD-03 adapter exposes one team as the single agent and plays every other team with a frozen `core.Agent`, here `ScriptedPrey`. Stable-Baselines3 PPO trains the predator through it with AD-10's settings, observation `(19,)` and action `(2,)`.
189	- **Alternatives:** The prey side — 6 agents, which the library can't run fairly. A multi-agent library — out of scope.
190	- **Consequences:** The adapter is a general "one learner, others frozen" wrapper, and the fish tank's single fish is its no-other-team case.
191	
192	### AD-16: Predator-prey reuses the tank's vectorised physics
193	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `envs`
194	- **Context:** HUNT-02 repeats FISH-02 and FISH-03's movement rules, and HUNT-09 needs the same seeded reproducibility. QR-07 gives each run 60 minutes, and the environment step is the inner loop.
195	- **Decision:** The swim-vector shortening, speed scaling, wall stop and seeded placement live as functions in `envs/tank.py`. `PredatorPrey` imports them and adds pellets, catches, respawns and the two views in `envs/predprey.py`, all vectorised over animals.
196	- **Alternatives:** A shared physics base class — inheritance for two users, against CR-12's "one implementation" rule only narrowly. Copying the functions — a duplicate pylint would flag (CR-42).
197	- **Consequences:** A change to the tank's movement changes both environments. Each keeps its own golden cases (FISH and HUNT) as the guard.
198	
199	### AD-17: Evaluation options travel as one `EvalOptions` value
200	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** `evaluate`, `train`, `reference`, `lab`
201	- **Context:** M1's interface task froze `evaluate.evaluate(make_env, agents, *, team, player, train_seed, n_games, record)`, which has 7 parameters. The bar caps a function at 5 (CR-20, ruff `PLR0913`), so the frozen signature fails the gate. `team`, `n_games` and `record` are how-to-evaluate settings with defaults that most callers keep. `player` and `train_seed` identify the `ScoreSet` and have no defaults.
202	- **Decision:** `evaluate.EvalOptions` is a frozen dataclass of `team` (default `SOLO`), `n_games` (200) and `record` (0). `evaluate` takes `make_env`, `agents`, `player`, `train_seed` and `options: EvalOptions = EvalOptions()`. It now has 5 parameters.
203	- **Alternatives:** A `PLR0913` exception for this one function — keeps the call sites short, but it's the first exception to a bar rule the project has had no other reason to bend. Folding `player` and `train_seed` into the options as well — one argument fewer, but the options would then stop being shareable between the players of one comparison.
204	- **Consequences:** Callers that keep the defaults don't change. Callers that set the team, game count or recordings build an `EvalOptions`. The frozen interface's type, samples and fake change with it, in a re-freeze interface task.
205	- **Probe:** none — an internal signature, no external API.
206	
207	### AD-18: `DQNAgent` takes its team's spec and the ablation name; `compare` takes chart paths
208	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** `agents`, `compare`, `lab`
209	- **Context:** M1's interface task found two more signatures the bar rejects. `agents.DQNAgent.__init__(obs_shape, n_actions, params, exploration, *, seed, use_memory, use_target)` has 7 parameters, over CR-20's 5. `compare.run_claims` took `charts: Sequence[viz.Chart]`, so `compare` would import `viz`, but the Components table and the import-linter layers keep `compare` and `viz` as siblings (CR-24). It only links the charts by path (EVAL-11).
210	- **Decision:** `DQNAgent` takes `team: TeamSpec` (it already carries the observation shape and the action count), `params`, `exploration`, `seed`, and `ablation: Literal["none", "no_memory", "no_target"]`, the same names `RunSettings.ablation` uses. `run_claims` takes `charts: Sequence[Path]`, the chart images to link.
211	- **Alternatives:** A `DQNSwitches(use_memory, use_target)` value — it still leaves 6 parameters beside `obs_shape` and `n_actions`. Letting `compare` depend on `viz` — a new layer edge for a path it only prints. Moving `viz.Chart` down into `runs` — a plotting type in the storage component.
212	- **Consequences:** `lab` builds `DQNAgent` from `env.teams[SOLO]` and passes `settings.ablation` through unchanged. `lab` reads the cross-play charts' paths from the report spec (`lab.ReportTargets.crossplay`). The frozen interface carries both signatures.
213	- **Probe:** none — internal signatures, no external API.
214	
215	## Risks and open questions
216	- **Full-grid timing (QR-03):** the CNN rung may need more than 60 CPU minutes to beat random. Mitigation: run the timing test first in that rung's milestone. Fall back to the flattened-grid MLP (AD-05), then to rented hardware within QR-05.
217	- **Scripted player too strong (EVAL-04):** every rung may tie the scripted player. Mitigation: reward and step-budget settings are per run (SNAKE-06), and the grid rung has the most room to beat it.
218	- **Matching the reference (REF-01):** Stable-Baselines3 has defaults the hand-written agents lack (DQN's gradient clipping at 10 and Huber loss; PPO's advantage normalisation and value-loss weight). Mitigation: the mapping in `reference` lists every one, and the hand-written agents adopt the same values, so a gap is the learner's code rather than a hidden setting.
219	- **Escape too easy or too hard (EVAL-07):** if fish at 0.8 × the ball's speed survive the cap easily, or never, the rungs can't separate from the scripted fish. Mitigation: the spec's numbers are tank constants; a measurement run of the two baselines comes first in S2's build, and a change to the numbers goes back through spec-writing.
220	- **The teams revision ripples into M1 (AD-13):** M1's pipelines and interface task were planned against one team per environment. Mitigation: they're revised and re-approved before `/build`; the expected values of TC-01–TC-49 don't change.
221	- **Self-play inside 60 minutes (QR-07):** two PPO learners sharing one run's budget may not reach a visible rung-2 failure, or a rung-3 fix. Mitigation: a timing run comes first in S3's build. The world numbers are spec constants, so any change goes back through spec-writing.
222	- **Cross-play cost:** 5,000 games of up to 1,000 steps with 7 animals is several minutes per run, and isn't covered by a QR. Mitigation: it's a separate command; measure it in S3's build and propose a QR if it hurts.
223	- **Package size:** 12 components and a contract of about 450 lines are at the Grow guides. AD-01 keeps one package. The natural seam, if one is needed, is `selfplay` with predator-prey.
224

105	class Finding(NamedTuple):
106	    rule: str
107	    path: str
108	    line: int  # 0 when the finding has no single line
109	    message: str
110	
111	    def __str__(self) -> str:
112	        where = f"{self.path}:{self.line}" if self.line else self.path
113	        return f"{where}: [{self.rule}] {self.message}"

116	@dataclass
117	class Diff:
118	    added: dict[str, list[tuple[int, str]]] = field(default_factory=dict)
119	    removed: dict[str, list[str]] = field(default_factory=dict)
120	    deleted: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
121	
122	    def added_lines(self, path: str) -> set[int]:
123	        return {n for n, _ in self.added.get(path, [])}
124
